An unambiguous prefix is that type (`show logging fra` → `frame`), `all` means no filter, and the filtered count is cross-checked against the full log. **Evidence:** `test/logging-filter.test.js` (19 assertions) |
| A group id was read as a moment in time | `show vrrp 1` answered **"VRRP at t=1 ms"** — the group id taken as one millisecond — and printed an unconverged table with no master for a group that `show vrrp` listed as **Master** with a virtual MAC: two views one word apart contradicting each other. `show ip ospf 99` answered "at t=99 ms" about the process that *does* exist, so a process id that is not running was silently answered anyway | **A trailing number is an instant only where the view has no other use for a number.** `show vrrp <group>` and `show ip ospf <process-id>` read it as what it names, filter to it, and refuse one that is not there while listing the ones that are. `at <ms>` and `converged` remain instants everywhere. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/instant-vs-argument.test.js` (29 assertions, including row-for-row agreement between `show vrrp` and `show vrrp <group>`) |
| Two views of one BGP peer, and only one could see it | `show ip bgp neighbors` said **"(no neighbours configured …)"** and `show ip bgp neighbors 10.0.0.2` said **"% No BGP neighbour … Configured: (none)"** — with advice to configure the peer that was already there — while `show ip bgp summary` listed that same peer. The summary read configuration; the detail views read only the *computed* session rows, so with no simulation run they saw nothing. `show ip protocols` answered **`% Invalid show command`** for a command whose facts the model already holds | The summary's rule now applies to every peer view (**a peer is configuration, its session state is not**), so a configured peer is `Idle / not yet evaluated` rather than missing. `show ip protocols` is implemented from the device's real interfaces and the single `Device.ADMIN_DISTANCE` table the route decision uses, so the distances cannot drift from the table they explain; on a host it names what the device *is*. `show standby` joins the absent-show table by naming VRRP. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/protocol-views.test.js` (33 assertions, including a cross-check that `show ip protocols` may not say "(none)" while `show ip ospf` lists an interface) |
| `show tech-support`, `show memory`, `show flash` … answered as typos | The families an operator reaches for **when a device misbehaves** were the ones still answering `% Invalid show command` — `show tech-support` most of all, since it is the first command typed when something is wrong. `show memory`, `buffers`, `controllers`, `diagnostics`, `flash`, `filesystems`, `boot`, `license`, `module` and `stack` were all in the same state | They join the existing absent-show table with sentences that name the absence **and** the views that answer the same question (`show tech-support` → version, running-config, interfaces counters, routing state, logging). No numbers are invented: a fabricated free-RAM figure is the same class of lie as the invented VLAN 1 rows. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/absent-commands.test.js` (133 assertions, including a sweep that extracts **every** command a refusal points at and runs it) |
| `do show` demoted the prompt out of every block | `do show …` inside an interface block answered the view and then **dropped the block** — the global handler's rule is *"a line I accept means you have left the block"*, right for `hostname`, wrong for a read. The ACL block and **both router modes** did the same (one `do show ip route` in an OSPF process, and the next `network` statement was read as global config); the DHCP pool **refused** `do show` instead | One helper (`_doShowInMode`) used by all five sub-modes: the view is answered and the prompt does not move. Plain `show <what>` is a read in router mode too, the router-mode page now lists it (`show <what> \| do show <what>`), and a **global** verb typed in a block still leaves the block — that rule is deliberate and unchanged. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/config-do-show.test.js` (21 assertions, each case proved by the *next configuration line* landing in the block) |
| The spanning tree could be configured per VLAN and not viewed that way | `spanning-tree vlan 10 root primary` is accepted and answers *"Spanning tree for VLAN 10"*, but **`show spanning-tree vlan 10` was refused** — *"% show spanning-tree takes one port, not \"vlan 10\""*, with advice about the port list, for a VLAN the previous prompt had just accepted. `show spanning-tree root` claimed **"Root bridge for VLAN 1"**, a per-VLAN specificity the model does not have, on a VLAN nobody configured | **The VLAN form answers with the tree that exists and says so**: *"VLAN 10 - one tree covers every VLAN on this device, so this is it"*, `root`/`summary` combine with `vlan <id>`, a missing VLAN is named with the ones that are present (*"The VLANs here are: 10, 20"*), and the root header without a VLAN speaks for **every VLAN** instead of inventing VLAN 1. The **capability gate runs before the VLAN lookup**, because a host was answering *"No VLAN 10 on PC1 … The VLANs here are: 1"* — re-inventing the VLAN 1 every other view had been taught to drop. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/stp-vlan-view.test.js` (17 assertions) plus the row on the exec page |
| `no vlan` blamed a port that was an SVI | A switch with its own address in VLAN 20 could not lose VLAN 20: **"% VLAN 20 is carried by Vlan20 … Move them first, e.g. \"switchport access vlan <other>\""** — `Vlan20` is an **SVI**, and there is no switchport to move it with, so the only instruction offered could not be carried out. The VLAN table made the same category error one layer down, listing the SVI in a column headed **Ports** | **Ports and SVIs are counted and reported separately** (`_vlanDependents` returns `svis`; `_vlanMap` marks the entry `Vlan20 (SVI)`): an SVI-only VLAN says *"is this device's own address: Vlan20 … Remove the interface first - \"no interface vlan 20\""*, a port-carried VLAN keeps the switchport advice, and both are named with their own remedy when both apply. The chain completes: `no interface vlan 20` → `no vlan 20` → gone from `show vlan`. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/svi-lifecycle.test.js` extended to 35 assertions, including that the SVI case does **not** mention `switchport` |
| A switch's own address could be created and never removed | Probing the SVI lifecycle (recent model work: `addSvi`, `betterRoute`) found the address functional — brief, connected route, ping — but **unremovable**: `no interface vlan 20` answered **"% Interface not found: vlan"** (the two-token spelling read "vlan" as a port name) and `no interface Vlan20` answered **"% Vlan20 is a physical interface and cannot be removed"**, which is true of nothing | **Both spellings remove the SVI**, report the address that went with it, and distinguish the two objects: the interface (the switch's address) and the VLAN (a bridge domain other ports carry) — *"VLAN 20 stays: Fa0/1 still carries it (remove with "no vlan 20")"*, or *"stays, empty"* when nothing carries it. Physical ports keep their refusal with a reason (*"A port is hardware, not configuration"*); a missing SVI is named as missing. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/svi-lifecycle.test.js` (26 assertions) covering creation, the up/down gate (no member port ⇒ no connected route), removal in both spellings, the missing-SVI and physical-port refusals, and the empty-VLAN message |
| The `clear` family, which read every argument as a port | IOS spells these with keywords; this console had only the port spelling, so **`clear mac address-table dynamic` answered "% No interface named dynamic on SW1"** and **`clear arp 10.0.0.9` answered "% No interface named 10.0.0.9 on R1"** — the argument *misread*, not missing. `clear mac address-table vlan 99` refused the keyword outright. A logging probe found `show logging summary last 3` printing **"Event summary (67 of 67 events)"** — the window parsed and then dropped on the summary branch | **Every documented form is parsed and narrows**: `dynamic`, `static` (which reports the table holds only learned addresses and points at port-security), `vlan <id>`, `interface <port>`, `address <mac>`; `clear arp` takes a port, an address, or `interface <port>`; a pinned ARP entry is refused with *"it is configuration, not a cache … Remove it with "no arp <ip>""* instead of being silently edited; a non-switch answers with the table it has. **`show logging summary last <n>`** summarises that window and says so in the header. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/clear-forms.test.js` (27 assertions) which checks the *table* after each clear via `show …`, not just the message |
| Filters that answered everything, and an ARP table that could not see its own row | Probing the `show` views with a **bogus argument** (does the answer change?) found `show access-lists [<name>]` documented on the page and on every help line, with the name **never read**: `show access-lists TEST` printed list 101, and `show access-lists NOSUCH` printed the identical full table — worse than no filter, because the operator reads it as *"yes, that list is here"*. A populated ARP probe found the port filter blind: `show arp Gi0/0` answered **"(none)"** while `arp 10.10.0.5 00:11:22:33:44:55` sat inside Gi0/0's subnet | **`show access-lists <name>` filters**, matching case-insensitively, refusing a missing name (*"% No access list NOSUCH on R1 … The lists here are: 101, VOICE"*) and sorting numeric lists before named ones (a name used to sort as `NaN`). **Pinned ARP entries get the port of their subnet** — a hand-written entry has no owner in the topology, so `show arp`, `show arp <port>` and `show arp <address>` now agree; an address outside every local subnet keeps `-` rather than inventing a port. **Evidence:** `engine/cli.js`, `test/show-filter.test.js` (25 assertions), including the pool rule the same probe surfaced — leaving `ip dhcp pool` without a range discards the half-built pool and says why |
